# Mutation campaign register

This register records every mutant that survived the whole-crate
`cargo mutants` campaign (FU-FUZ-01): one row per `missed` or `timeout`
outcome, keyed by the campaign SHA and the full cargo-mutants name with its
location. `scripts/lint-gates/mutants_report.py --register` reads the table at
the end of this file; [`../lint-policy.md`](../lint-policy.md) describes the
schema and the final statuses (`killed` with the test that kills it,
`equivalent` with the reason no caller can observe the change, `hang` for a
timeout that never completes on its own: it still hangs in a 600 s local
re-run, or that re-run ends only when the mutant's unbounded allocation hits
the 8 GiB address-space cap of the local runs). Every row starts `open`; the
triage replaces the status and the proof, and each verification campaign
moves the rows to its own SHA. The `outcome` column keeps the outcome of the
campaign that opened the row; a row whose mutant is missed or timed out in the
current campaign carries that outcome.

## Campaign

- Commit: `d7666e73b0ba46c093e74a69599c25506cd0582a` (`main`), the final tree
  after critic round 5 (`f77562b`) with the tests of its mutation check.
- CI run: [`42727860783`](https://github.com/andrico21/rskrb5-ng/actions/runs/42727860783)
  (`workflow_dispatch`), jobs `mutants (0)` to `mutants (31)`, each run once.
- Command per shard: `timeout -k 5m 170m cargo mutants --all-features
  --no-shuffle --cap-lints=true --shard k/32 -j 2` with cargo-mutants 27.1.0,
  `CARGO_BUILD_WARNINGS=allow`, no debug info, and the run confined to a
  cgroup with 12 GiB of memory and no swap. The per-mutant timeout is the one
  cargo-mutants derives from each shard's baseline test time (194 s to 334 s
  in this run).
- Completeness and triage: `mutants_report.py --artifacts <the 32 shard
  artifacts> --expect-shards 32 --register docs/review/mutants.md --campaign
  d7666e73b0ba46c093e74a69599c25506cd0582a --enforce` prints `mutants:
  caught=3839 missed=59 timeout=3 unviable=1178 success=0` and
  `0 problem(s)`.

## Totals

| Outcome | Mutants |
| --- | ---: |
| caught | 3839 |
| missed | 59 |
| timeout | 3 |
| unviable | 1178 |
| total | 5079 |

These are the outcomes CI recorded. The register has 682 rows: 619 `killed`,
59 `equivalent` and 4 `hang`; 568 were opened by a missed mutant and 114 by a
timeout.

## Verification

### In-diff check after the policy fixes (todo 39)

Todos 31 to 36 changed `src/` after the campaign above (POL-01 to POL-06,
SEC-01, SEC-02 and ORA-07: request DER bound to the authorized message,
additional tickets checked, raw encoders refusing prohibited enctypes, staged
request lists refused instead of narrowed, the `includedir` depth cap, KDC
discovery admitted through `restrictions::config`, and the destructuring
`Debug` impls). The changed code was checked with `cargo mutants --in-diff`.

- Diff: `git diff f77562b 68fb2d6 -- src` (11 files). `f77562b` is the code
  campaign `d7666e7` covered (`git diff f77562b d7666e7 -- src` is empty);
  `68fb2d6` is `main` after todo 38.
- CI's `mutants` job cannot take a diff (`workflow_dispatch` declares no
  inputs), so the check ran locally with the job's flags: `cargo mutants
  --all-features --no-shuffle --cap-lints=true --in-diff <diff> --shard k/4 -j 2
  --copy-target=false` at `68fb2d6`, cargo-mutants 27.1.0,
  `CARGO_BUILD_WARNINGS=allow`, no debug info and an 8 GiB address-space cap.
  Each shard measured its own baseline (48 s to 50 s build, 64 s to 65 s test),
  and cargo-mutants set the timeout from it (325 s to 326 s).
- `mutants_report.py --artifacts <the 4 shard outputs> --expect-shards 4
  --register docs/review/mutants.md --campaign
  68fb2d64aa5b9def9698f9720cb80b791f754107 --enforce` prints `mutants:
  caught=21 missed=0 timeout=0 unviable=21 success=0` and `0 problem(s)`.

| Shard | Mutants | Caught | Missed | Timeout | Unviable | Wall time (s) |
| ---: | ---: | ---: | ---: | ---: | ---: | ---: |
| 0 | 11 | 0 | 0 | 0 | 11 | 182 |
| 1 | 11 | 8 | 0 | 0 | 3 | 318 |
| 2 | 11 | 11 | 0 | 0 | 0 | 441 |
| 3 | 9 | 2 | 0 | 0 | 7 | 216 |

No mutant survived, so the check adds no register row. The 21 unviable
mutants return `Default::default()` for types without a `Default`
(`BuiltTgsReq`, `AsReq`, `TgsReq`, `AsRepSession`, `TgsRepSession`,
`KdcEndpoint`), or call `Zeroizing` constructors that do not exist.

cargo-mutants does not mutate files that are pulled in with `include!`. The
diff changes one of them, `src/config/enctype_policy.rs` (included by
`src/config.rs`), whose new `validate_request_enctypes` therefore had no
mutant in this or any earlier campaign. It was checked in a scratch copy with
the file inlined into `src/config.rs` (`cargo mutants --all-features
--no-shuffle --cap-lints=true --file src/config.rs --re
validate_request_enctypes -j 2`). Both mutants were caught. The replacement
`Ok(())` failed the client tests
`as_req_with_disjoint_permitted_enctypes_fails_closed`,
`as_req_rejects_mixed_disabled_enctype_list` and
`tgs_req_rejects_mixed_disabled_enctype_list`. The deleted `!` failed 12 library
tests, among them
`checked_derivation_enforces_floors_before_pbkdf2`.

### Final tree after critic round 5 (todo 22r)

Critic round 5 (`f77562b`) made the credential owners private, with
accessors, setters, redacted `Debug` impls and wipes, and changed admission and
configuration code; it touched the files of 300 of the 552 rows. The register
was moved to the new tree, and every change was checked by a full campaign. It
took two rounds.

**Remap.** The 552 rows of `7343510` were matched to the 5079 mutants of
`f77562b` by the method of todo 21: 514 on the mutated text with an identical
context, 27 on the mutated text alone, and 11 by inspecting the diffs. In 9 of
those 11, round 5 replaced field reads by accessor calls
(`header_ref().to_owned()`, `padata_mut()`, `etype()`, `sequence_number()`,
`subkey()`) around the unchanged mutated expression;
`src/config/section_decode.rs:132:49` has a second candidate because round 5
added the same check to the `[capaths]` arm (the `[plugins]` one is
`124:49`); and the `KeyBytes` erasure gained a test hook. No row was retired.
The mutant set of `d7666e7` is identical to that of `f77562b` (same names and
diffs), so both rounds use one map.

**Round 1** (`f77562b`, run
[`42676503835`](https://github.com/andrico21/rskrb5-ng/actions/runs/42676503835)):
complete, with 3716 caught, 180 missed, 4 timeout, 1179 unviable and 17 memory
kills. Every `equivalent` row was missed again and every `killed` row was
caught; two `hang` rows timed out and the third (`src/crypto.rs:2028:5`) was
caught through the OOM killer. 129 new mutants survived:

- 123 are killed by new or extended tests (commits `a27ef34`, `483d1e2`,
  `ce8974e` and `000fb8a`; tests only). Nearly all mutate code that round 5
  added: accessors and setters of the now private owners, which no test read
  back; the redacted `Debug` impls, whose audit only checked that secrets were
  absent, which an empty rendering satisfies (the audit now also requires a
  non-empty rendering, and renders `NegotiateLayer` and `NegotiateService`);
  and the validation-info scalars and NTLM flags of the PAC. Three findings
  stand out: `random_nonce` (`&` to `^`) was caught at `7343510` only by
  chance, by two tests with 8 random draws and 1, and the test now takes 64;
  the S4U2Proxy option bit is now set through an accessor expression, whose
  `^` differs only when the caller already set the bit; and the two
  `AsRepSession` time setters timed out because password-change tests waited
  forever for a client that never connected, so the mock KDC accept now fails
  after 10 s.
- 6 are equivalent: the three PAC `version` accessors, whose structures only
  `parse` builds, and `parse` admits only version 0; and the `Drop` impls of
  `InitiatorContext`, `MicToken` and `WrapToken`, which erase fields that
  either erase themselves (`Zeroizing`) or are sent on the wire as they are,
  with no hook a test could observe.

**Round 2** (`d7666e7`, run
[`42727860783`](https://github.com/andrico21/rskrb5-ng/actions/runs/42727860783)):
complete, with 3839 caught, 59 missed, 3 timeout, 1178 unviable and 15 memory
kills. Every `equivalent` row was missed, every `killed` row was caught
(including the 123 new ones), two `hang` rows timed out and the third was
caught through the OOM killer again. One mutant timed out that round 1 and
`7343510` had recorded caught through the OOM killer: `src/crypto.rs:1852:5`
(`aes_cts_encrypt` returning `Ok((vec![1], vec![]))`), a runaway allocation
that ends locally only at the 8 GiB address-space cap. It is a new `hang` row,
with the CI timeout and the capped local re-run as evidence. No test changed in
this round, so no third round was needed.

### Final tree (todo 22)

The critic rounds of todo 22 changed the code after `4d3995d`. The register
was moved to the final tree, and every change was checked by a full campaign,
a superset of `cargo mutants --in-diff` over the todo-22 diff. It took two
rounds.

**Remap.** The 531 rows of `4d3995d` were matched to the mutants of `aa75616`
by the method of todo 21: 469 on the mutated text with an identical context,
18 on the mutated text alone, and 28 by inspecting the diffs. Those 28 keep
the function and the replacement while the body changed, or keep the
mutated expression, which `38cdd37` moved from `State::line` into the new
`State::content`. The other 16 rows are retired because todo 22 deleted or
rewrote the code they mutated ("Retired rows" below). The mutant set of
`7343510` is identical to that of `aa75616` (4254 mutants, same names and
diffs), so both rounds use one map.

**Round 1** (`aa75616`, run
[`42514093535`](https://github.com/andrico21/rskrb5-ng/actions/runs/42514093535)):
incomplete. Shard 16 was cut off at the 170-minute wall after 103 of its 133
mutants. The parity-manifest tests of todo 22 had raised the baseline test
time from about a minute to 194 to 365 s, because for every manifest symbol
they re-ran the source lexer and rescanned every source file. Mutants whose
killing tests live in `tests/pac.rs` or `tests/service.rs` run those binaries
first, so shard 16 ran about four times longer. Commit `2fb0397` resolves the
evidence once per checkout; the baseline is 35 to 50 s in round 2. In the 31
complete shards every `equivalent` row was missed again, both `hang` rows
timed out, and 37 new mutants survived, all in code that todo 22 added or
changed:

- 34 are killed by new or extended tests (commits `738e150`, `efdab24`,
  `3fdc612`, `c30b20f` and `1462c33`). They mutate the new redacting `Debug`
  impls, whose tests only checked that secrets were absent, which an empty
  rendering satisfies; the serde payload visitor of the credential cache; the
  version-3 size estimate of `CCache::to_bytes`;
  `Keytab::add_entry_from_password_ref`; `KeyBytes` erasure; the package
  lookup of `restrictions::reject`; and the libdefaults and plugin
  classifications that a later admission check repeats, whose arms show only
  in which restriction is named first.
- 3 are equivalent: two bound computations inside a `#[cfg(test)]` capacity
  assertion of `cts_decrypt`, and an arm of `restrictions::reject` whose
  fallback finds the same package.

One `killed` row became `equivalent`: `38cdd37` made the disjunct mutated by
`src/config/libdefaults.rs:23:45` unreachable.

**Round 2** (`7343510`, run
[`42541698874`](https://github.com/andrico21/rskrb5-ng/actions/runs/42541698874)):
complete, with 3358 caught, 53 missed, 3 timeout, 840 unviable and 15 memory
kills. Every `equivalent` row was missed, every `hang` row timed out, the 34
new `killed` rows were caught, and every other `killed` row was caught except
one, which is now `hang` (below).

**Runaway allocations.** Seven `killed` rows of `4d3995d` had the CI outcome
`timeout`: `src/crypto/scratch.rs:63:9` and six `encrypt_data` and
`des3_cbc_encrypt` replacements that make key derivation allocate without
bound. A test fails under each, but sibling tests spin until the per-mutant
timeout, unless the cgroup's OOM killer ends the test binary first, which CI
records as caught. Locally each is caught because the leak hits the 8 GiB
address-space cap. At `7343510` the scratch mutant is caught in 5 s by named
library tests, without a spin; five rows are caught through the OOM killer;
and `src/crypto.rs:2028:5` (`des3_cbc_encrypt` returning `Ok((vec![],
vec![]))`) timed out, so it is now `hang`, with the CI timeout and the capped
local re-run as evidence. Which of these rows times out varies from run to
run, so each proof records the CI outcomes of every campaign.

### Verification campaign (todo 21)

The first complete campaign (`91b09c2`, below) left 528 rows. Todos 19 and
20 triaged them to 471 `killed`, 55 `equivalent` and 2 `hang`. The
verification campaign took two rounds.

**Remap.** Each row was matched to exactly one mutant of the new tree, by its
file, its name without the location and its diff. 515 rows matched on the
mutated text with an identical context and 5 on the mutated text alone (the
context had changed). The other 8 were matched by inspecting the diffs: todos
19 and 20 had changed the enclosing body. `Tokio::get_service_ticket` became
`Tokio::get_service_ticket_with_clock`, the two cached-TGT helpers take a
`now` argument, the NTLM `Drop` gained a test hook, and
`random_sequence_number` imports `fill`. 128 rows changed location, and three
of them changed function as well. The mutant set of `4d3995d` is identical to
that of `be8ebb4` (4126 mutants, same names and diffs), so both rounds use one
map.

**Round 1** (`be8ebb4`, run
[`42346561706`](https://github.com/andrico21/rskrb5-ng/actions/runs/42346561706)):
caught 3160, missed 56, timeout 93, unviable 817, 11 memory kills. Every
`equivalent` row was missed again and both `hang` rows timed out; 383 `killed`
rows were caught, 88 timed out, and 4 new mutants survived.

- 85 of the 88 hung in the whole suite. Todos 19 and 20 had proved these kills
  with a filtered run. In the whole suite the library's test binary still had
  a failing test, but `configured_auto_retains_tcp_stream` and the two UDP
  `oversized` tests waited forever for a peer the mutant never contacted, so
  the binary never exited. Commit `95445a1` bounds those peers at 10 s; all
  85 are caught in a local whole-suite run (test phase about 10 s).
- 3 make the key derivation loop never progress
  (`src/crypto/scratch.rs:63:9` and two `Des3CbcSha1KdEtype::encrypt_data`
  replacements). A test fails, but sibling tests spin in the mutated loop
  until the per-mutant timeout, and no test-side bound interrupts a
  synchronous loop. These rows stay `killed` with that evidence in the proof.
  A local whole-suite run is caught because the leak hits the 8 GiB
  address-space cap of the local runs. CI's cgroup has no address-space cap.
- New: `src/service/replay_quota.rs:50:23` was missed. At `91b09c2` an
  unrelated, timing-dependent test had caught it. It is a real gap: the
  mutant drops a client's quota entry while records are live, so a partial
  expiry hands the client a fresh share.
  `partial_expiry_keeps_the_remaining_share_counted` (commit `4d3995d`) kills
  it. The three `aes_cts_encrypt` replacements timed out. At `91b09c2` the
  cgroup had OOM-killed them, so they counted as caught. They belong to the
  previous class: `aes_cts_encrypts_and_decrypts_rfc3962_vectors` fails, and
  sibling tests spin.

**Round 2** (`4d3995d`, run
[`42356845559`](https://github.com/andrico21/rskrb5-ng/actions/runs/42356845559)):
caught 3247, missed 55, timeout 9, unviable 815, 9 memory kills. Every
`equivalent` row was missed again, both `hang` rows timed out and 466 `killed`
rows were caught; no fixture hang is left, and the replay-quota mutant is
caught. All 9 timeouts belong to the looping class: the two `hang` rows; five
`killed` rows (`src/crypto/scratch.rs:63:9`, two
`Des3CbcSha1KdEtype::encrypt_data` and two `des3_cbc_encrypt` replacements;
the last two had been OOM-killed, so caught, in round 1); and two new rows,
`des3_cbc_encrypt` and `AesSha1Etype::encrypt_data` returning `Ok((vec![1],
vec![]))`, which the cgroup had OOM-killed in both earlier campaigns. Each of
these seven `killed` rows names a test that fails under its mutant, and its
proof records the CI timeout and the local evidence. With the rows moved to
`4d3995d`, the register passed `mutants_report.py --enforce`.

### Earlier campaigns

- `f77562b` (32 shards, run `42676503835`): round 1 of the check after critic
  round 5, above. Caught 3716, missed 180, timeout 4, unviable 1179 of 5079
  mutants.
- `7343510` (32 shards, run `42541698874`): round 2 of the final-tree
  campaign of todo 22. Caught 3358, missed 53, timeout 3, unviable 840 of 4254
  mutants. Job `mutants (7)` was re-run once at the same commit: in attempt 1
  the OOM killer, triggered by a runaway mutant running in parallel in the
  same cgroup, killed the cargo process building `src/crypto.rs:329:9`, which
  cargo-mutants records with the summary `Failure` that `mutants_report.py`
  rejects.
- `aa75616` (32 shards, run `42514093535`): round 1 of the final-tree
  campaign, above. In the 31 complete shards (4121 mutants): caught 3226,
  missed 86, timeout 2, unviable 806 and one `Failure` summary; shard 16
  stopped at 103 of 133 mutants.
- `4d3995d` (32 shards, run `42356845559`): round 2 of the todo-21
  verification. Caught 3247, missed 55, timeout 9, unviable 815 of 4126
  mutants.
- `be8ebb4` (32 shards, run `42346561706`): round 1 of the todo-21 verification,
  above.
- `91b09c2` (32 shards, run `41348118329`): the first complete campaign.
  Caught 2779, missed 418, timeout 110, unviable 818 of 4125 mutants. The
  cgroup's OOM killer ended 14 processes. Four of those mutants were recorded
  unviable because the killed process was a `rustc` building them, and all 14
  were caught when re-tested alone. Its 528 missed and timed-out mutants are
  the rows of this register.
- `7953988` (8 shards, run `40623509423`): caught 1813, missed 288, timeout 76,
  unviable 641 over the 2818 mutants it reached. Shard 7 uploaded nothing and
  shards 2 and 5 stopped at 359 and 394 of 413 mutants, so the campaign was
  incomplete. The crate has changed a great deal since, so the counts are not
  comparable one to one.
- `38970af` (16 shards, run `41325372774`): incomplete. Shards 9, 11 and 14
  were cut off at the 170-minute wall, and the runners of shards 0, 3, 4, 5
  and 15 were lost mid-run. The losses came from mutants that allocate without
  bound and starve the runner before their timeout fires (see "Memory kills"),
  so the mutation job now runs 32 shards without debug info, inside a memory
  cgroup (`.github/workflows/ci.yml`, commits `5fef86f` and `fd58097`).

### Memory kills

The cgroup's OOM killer ended 15 processes in this campaign, in shards 1, 7,
8, 9 and 30. Each matches one mutant whose log shows the SIGKILL. CI recorded
14 of them as caught, because the killed process was a test binary, and one
(`src/crypto.rs:1049:9` returning `Ok((vec![], vec![1]))`) as unviable,
because the killed process was a `rustc` building it. Round 1 (`f77562b`) had
17 kills: the same 15, `src/crypto.rs:1062:9` (unviable there) and
`src/crypto.rs:1852:5` returning `Ok((vec![1], vec![]))`, which timed out in
round 2. All 17 were re-tested alone at `ce8974e`, whose `src/` is that of
`d7666e7` (`cargo mutants --all-features --no-shuffle --cap-lints=true
--copy-target=false --jobs 1 --baseline=skip --timeout 600 --re '^<name>$'`,
`ulimit -v 8388608`): all 17 are caught, 15 by an allocation failure at the
8 GiB address-space cap and 2 by failing library tests in 2.5 s.

| Mutant | CI outcome at `d7666e7` | Alone |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `src/ccache.rs:1714:5: replace read_array -> Result<[u8; N], Error> with Ok([0; N])` | caught | caught (allocation abort) |
| `src/crypto.rs:1049:9: replace Des3CbcSha1KdEtype::encrypt_data -> Result<(Vec<u8>, Vec<u8>), Error> with Ok((vec![0], vec![]))` | caught | caught (allocation abort) |
| `src/crypto.rs:1049:9: replace Des3CbcSha1KdEtype::encrypt_data -> Result<(Vec<u8>, Vec<u8>), Error> with Ok((vec![1], vec![]))` | caught | caught (allocation abort) |
| `src/crypto.rs:1049:9: replace Des3CbcSha1KdEtype::encrypt_data -> Result<(Vec<u8>, Vec<u8>), Error> with Ok((vec![], vec![1]))` | unviable | caught (test failure) |
| `src/crypto.rs:1049:9: replace Des3CbcSha1KdEtype::encrypt_data -> Result<(Vec<u8>, Vec<u8>), Error> with Ok((vec![], vec![]))` | caught | caught (allocation abort) |
| `src/crypto.rs:1062:9: replace Des3CbcSha1KdEtype::decrypt_data -> Result<Vec<u8>, Error> with Ok(vec![])` | caught | caught (test failure) |

The same class recurs in every campaign, but which of these mutants the cgroup
kills, and which run into the per-mutant timeout instead, varies from run to
run: `91b09c2` had 14 kills, `be8ebb4` 11, `4d3995d` 9, `7343510` 15,
`f77562b` 17 and `d7666e7` 15, and every one was caught when re-tested alone.

## Shards

Wall time is the cargo-mutants run (`start_time` to `end_time` of the shard's
`outcomes.json`); the job time adds checkout, the cargo-mutants install and the
upload. Every shard finished well inside the 170-minute wall (at most 100.8
minutes).
